Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this, but... mostly I’m here because I’m just so tired lately.

I know everyone gets run down, so I'm sure it's nothing major, but even just doing little things around the house, like unloading the dishwasher, or trying to read for my certificate class... it just feels like too much right now. I'm trying my best to push through it, I promise. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay.

Um... well, maybe not quite as much as I usually do? I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, because I know I should be keeping busy, but... yeah, I guess I’ve sort of stepped back from things lately.

Um... well, I haven't been weighing myself or anything, so I really hope it hasn't been a significant amount. I always try to take good care of myself and eat right, I really do!

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... no, definitely not most of the time. I really try not to use that word, "depressed." It just sounds so... negative, you know? And I really want to be a positive person.

Um... well, no, I wouldn't say there's a persistent sadness. I really try not to let things get to me like that.

Um... well, it's been... maybe a little while? But it's not like I've been doing absolutely nothing, I promise. I still get up and try to be productive. 

I guess this... this quieter stretch, where I'm just not as interested in things and feeling a bit more tired than usual... it's been going on for maybe about three months? 

But I've been trying so hard to push through it, you know? I really don't want it to seem like I'm just letting myself go or being ungrateful for my life. Three months sounds like a long time when I say it out loud, but I've been doing my best to manage it and still be a good wife. That's... that's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just trying to keep going even when you're tired?

Um... well, I haven't actually stepped on a scale, so I don't know the exact percentage... but I really don't want you to think I'm starving myself on purpose or trying to be unhealthy. I know how important it is to take care of my body.

Um... well, like I was mentioning a few minutes ago... I guess if I really think about it, yes, it has been every single day lately. 

I know I brought up my sketching, and the gallery openings, and cooking and my yoga... and tennis with my friend. It's just... It's just a little temporary rut with my hobbies, which is... that's a pretty normal thing to go through, isn't it? Just a little phase where you don't want to do your everyday things? I'm sure my interest will come back any day now, as long as I keep trying.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this, but... mostly I’m here because I’m just so tired lately. \n\nBut it's, um, it's in a way that sleep doesn’t really seem to fix? I know everyone gets run down, so I'm sure it's nothing major, but even just doing little things around the house, like unloading the dishwasher, or trying to read for my certificate class... it just feels like too much right now. I'm trying my best to push through it, I promise. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe not quite as much as I usually do? I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, because I know I should be keeping busy, but... yeah, I guess I’ve sort of stepped back from things lately. \n\nUsually I love sketching, or going to gallery openings... cooking, doing yoga, or even just playing tennis with a friend. But right now, I just haven't really cared about doing any of that. It’s like I’ve stopped caring about the things that usually make me feel like myself. I’m sure it’s just a temporary slump, though, right? I hope that doesn't sound too terrible...\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I don't think I'd use the word sad? I mean, I really hope that doesn't contradict what I just said about my hobbies—I don't want to be confusing! But I definitely don't sit around crying or feeling miserable, I promise. I always try to keep a positive attitude. \n\nIt’s more just... feeling a bit blank, I guess? Or just really drained, like I mentioned before. So I wouldn't say I feel down most of the time. Is it okay if I explain it like that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to experience pleasure when you do things?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I think so? I really try to find the good in things, you know? I don't want to sound like I'm just totally numb or ungrateful for what I have. \n\nWhen I do push myself to, say, watch a movie or have dinner with my partner, it's... it's fine. It's nice. I guess maybe the spark isn't quite as bright as it usually is, but I wouldn't say I can't feel pleasure at all. I definitely still appreciate things. I hope that makes sense? I just don't want you to think I'm completely closed off to enjoying life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard to do the right thing, so I don't usually have a reason to feel guilty, you know? I hope that doesn't sound arrogant! I just... I always try to be considerate of others and pull my own weight. \n\nI mean, maybe a little guilty that I haven't been as active lately, like I mentioned before? But not, like, a deep, terrible guilt about everything I've ever done. I try not to dwell on things like that. Is that the kind of guilt you mean? I just want to make sure I'm answering correctly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite lately?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say there's a huge change, really. I always try to make sure I'm eating healthy and taking care of myself, I promise. \n\nI guess... maybe my appetite has been just a little bit off lately? Sometimes I'll get to the end of the day and realize I've only really had coffee and maybe a few bites of something, but it's not because I'm starving myself or anything like that! I just sort of forget to eat because I'm not feeling super hungry. And I did notice my jeans are fitting a bit looser, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight. I hope that's not too concerning? I'm definitely still trying to make sure I eat when I can. Does that answer what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving or speaking more slowly than you usually do?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I try really hard to keep up with everything, I promise. I definitely don't want to sound like I'm just being lazy or anything! \n\nBut... I guess, maybe on my slower days, it kind of feels like my body is moving through wet cement? Just a little bit heavy. And sometimes my partner will ask me a really simple question, and it's like... it just takes me a weirdly long time to get the words out to answer them. I do always answer, though! It just takes a minute to gather my thoughts. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound, I don't know, incompetent? I'm still trying my best to stay on top of things and be present. Does that sound like what you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or a failure?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not. I mean, I really try to stay positive and look for the good in things, you know? I wouldn't say I feel like a failure at all. \n\nI mean, sure, everyone has days where they wish they'd done something better, right? Like, maybe I could be doing more with my time since I'm not working right now, but... I always try to remind myself that I'm doing my best. I really do try to be a good person and contribute where I can. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like I'm in denial or anything! I just... I don't walk around feeling worthless. I try to keep my chin up. Is that... is that a good way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it hasn't been forever or anything like that. I really do try to keep things on track and not let myself get stuck in a rut, I promise. \n\nI guess... if I really think about it, this whole pattern has been going on for maybe, um, about four months? It sort of started around the time my spouse and I started having a lot more arguments. Not that it's their fault at all! We're just... communicating a bit differently lately, I guess. I really try to be a good partner, and I know relationships have their ups and downs, so I don't want to make it sound like things are terrible at home. \n\nI just started noticing I was feeling a bit more off around then. I hope I'm not overreacting by bringing that up? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline. Does four months sound like what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any days over the past two weeks where you woke up feeling pretty good, or at least not feeling unwell?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really wish I could say yes, because I know I should be having good days. I try so hard to wake up with a positive attitude, I really do! But... if I'm being completely honest, I don't think there's been a day like that recently. \n\nIt's just been a bit of a struggle to feel like myself when I wake up. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or not trying to get better, because I am! I always try to push through it. But yeah, the last couple of weeks... it's just been consistently kind of heavy. \n\nI hope it's okay that I haven't had any good days to report? I don't want you to think I'm not putting in the effort to feel better. Is it normal to not have a break in it for a couple of weeks?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel sad for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to let myself get down like that. I know it's important to stay positive, especially since I'm home most of the day. \n\nI might have moments where I feel a little blue, but I always try to snap myself out of it, you know? I'll put on some music or try to find something productive to do around the house. I definitely wouldn't say I'm sad for most of the day. I really try to be pleasant to be around for my spouse when they get home. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just don't want to seem like I'm wallowing or anything. I really do try to keep a good attitude.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I haven't been weighing myself or anything, so I really hope it hasn't been a significant amount. I always try to take good care of myself and eat right, I really do! \n\nBut... I guess my appetite has been just a little bit off lately. Sometimes it gets late in the day and I'll realize I've really only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. I definitely don't do it on purpose, I promise! I just sort of forget to eat, I guess. And... well, I did notice recently that my jeans have gotten a bit looser, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight at all. \n\nI always try to make sure I eat a good dinner when my spouse gets home, though, so I'm really trying to keep my strength up. Is it okay that my clothes are fitting a little differently? I hope that doesn't make it seem like I'm doing a bad job taking care of myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in a low mood for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I wouldn't say that. I really try to keep my spirits up as best I can. I know I mentioned things feeling a bit heavy earlier, but I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around moping all day. \n\nI try to keep myself busy, you know? I'll tidy up the house or find little projects to do so I'm not just dwelling on things. I really want to make sure I'm creating a nice environment for when my spouse gets home, so I do my best to shake off any low feelings. \n\nI might have moments where I feel a little flat, but I really try not to let it take over the whole day. I hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right things to try and feel better.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um... no, definitely not most of the time. I really try not to use that word, \"depressed.\" It just sounds so... negative, you know? And I really want to be a positive person. \n\nI mean, everyone has days where they feel a little less energetic, right? But I always make sure I get up and get dressed. I don't just stay in bed. I want to make sure I'm being a good partner and keeping things nice around here. \n\nI really do my best to focus on the good things. I hope it doesn't sound like I'm complaining too much about feeling a little off sometimes. I'm really trying to handle it well, is that... is that the right way to look at it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's a persistent sadness inside you, even if it's not something you'd call feeling 'down'?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, I wouldn't say there's a persistent sadness. I really try not to let things get to me like that. \n\nI mean, sometimes I guess I feel a little... I don't know, quiet inside? But it's definitely not a deep sadness or anything that sticks around all the time. I always try to find something to smile about, especially when my husband is around. I don't want to bring him down, you know? \n\nI really do try to focus on the brighter side of things. I hope that's the right way to handle it... I'm doing okay by trying to stay positive, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy most of the time over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, maybe just a little bit? But I really don't want you to think I'm just giving up on things or being lazy. \n\nI guess I have... kind of stopped doing some of the things that usually make me feel like myself. Like, normally I really love sketching, or going to gallery openings, and... I just haven't really cared about doing them lately. Same with cooking, or my yoga, or even playing tennis with my friend. I've sort of let all of that slide the last couple of weeks. \n\nBut I think I just needed a little break, you know? With everything else I try to keep up with, maybe I just needed to rest from my hobbies for a minute. It's perfectly normal to take a step back and recharge sometimes, isn't it? I'm sure I'll get right back into all of it soon. I just hope it's okay that I haven't been as active with those things... I really am trying my best.\n\nDoctor: For how long have you not been enjoying the activities you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's been... maybe a little while? But it's not like I've been doing absolutely nothing, I promise. I still get up and try to be productive. \n\nI guess this... this quieter stretch, where I'm just not as interested in things and feeling a bit more tired than usual... it's been going on for maybe about three months? \n\nBut I've been trying so hard to push through it, you know? I really don't want it to seem like I'm just letting myself go or being ungrateful for my life. Three months sounds like a long time when I say it out loud, but I've been doing my best to manage it and still be a good wife. That's... that's the right thing to do, isn't it? Just trying to keep going even when you're tired?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past four months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I haven't actually stepped on a scale, so I don't know the exact percentage... but I really don't want you to think I'm starving myself on purpose or trying to be unhealthy. I know how important it is to take care of my body. \n\nIt's just... I guess my appetite has been a little off lately. Sometimes I'll get to the end of the day and realize I've only had coffee and maybe a few bites of something. And... well, my jeans have definitely gotten a bit looser, even though I haven't been trying to lose weight at all. \n\nBut I always make sure to sit down and have dinner when my husband gets home, so I promise I am eating! It's just so easy to forget during the day when things are quiet and I'm not doing as much. That happens to everyone sometimes, right? As long as I'm still making an effort at dinner, I'm doing okay, aren't I?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy every day over the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I was mentioning a few minutes ago... I guess if I really think about it, yes, it has been every single day lately. \n\nI know I brought up my sketching, and the gallery openings, and cooking and my yoga... and tennis with my friend. It's just... I really have stopped caring about all those things that usually make me feel like myself. Every day I wake up and I tell myself, 'Today I'll do some yoga or cook a nice meal,' but then the day just slips by and I haven't touched any of it. \n\nBut I really don't want to sound like a broken record, or like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself! I'm still making sure the house is tidy and everything. It's just a little temporary rut with my hobbies, which is... that's a pretty normal thing to go through, isn't it? Just a little phase where you don't want to do your everyday things? I'm sure my interest will come back any day now, as long as I keep trying.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Um... yes, I suppose it really has been most of the day, every day. 

Like I was saying, it's just... those things I usually love, like my sketching, or going to the gallery openings, cooking... even my yoga and tennis. I just... I haven't cared about them at all. From the time I wake up until I go to sleep, I just can't seem to find that spark for any of it.
